# Parity: Bar (bottom island taskbar and all its popovers)
Baseline: origin/master 6ca499f (2026-11-06). Inventory by lane native-plan-inv-bar.
Rows: 1045   Scope: ui/bar/** (except corners.*), lib/{bar-follow,bar-intent,bar-shape,pinned,tray-batch,winlist-icons,icon-fresh,windows,helm-badge,media-pick,media-guard,quick-links,net-probe,sys-stats,thumbs,mantle-apps,quartz-exe,spotify-launch,notifications}.js, lib/features/{qstiles,jumplist,helmbadge,downloads,gamemode,powermode,quicklinks,gcal,ontop,spotify(widget)}*.js, and the bar's IPC/lifecycle code in main.js.

## Bar shell: shape, geometry, reveal, hover, glass, state

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |

| BAR-001 | Bar / Shape | Default shape is the floating pill (island): a 48 DIP bar centred in its window with a 14 DIP transparent strip below it (the gap under the island); `--flush` (without `--pill`) gives the full-width docked edge strip instead; anything unknown falls back to edge | main.js (BAR_SHAPE, flags.pill/flags.flush), ui/bar/bar.js#setMode, ui/bar/bar.css (body.pill .bar, body.edge .bar, --lift) | ui/bar/bar.page.test.js (reveal) | todo |

| BAR-162 | Bar / Explorer restart | The auto-hide flag changed under Mantle (Explorer restart, or the user in Windows Settings): the sidecar's `taskbar` event is trusted and written to State.taskbar; an exe that sends the old {hidden} shape gives "the sidecar sent a taskbar event in the old shape (...), so Mantle does not know the auto-hide flag: run npm run build:sidecar" | main.js sidecar.on('taskbar') | none | todo |

| BAR-164 | Bar / Edge clip | The 2 px edge clip is re-applied ~150 ms after every foreground change, plus a 1 s backstop (Windows drops any cursor clip on a foreground change, lock, UAC, Ctrl+Alt+Del) | sidecar/EdgeClip.cs | sidecar/test-sidecar.js | todo |
| BAR-165 | Bar / Edge clip | The edge clip yields to: another app's clip (smaller than the monitor and not Mantle's), a full-screen window in front, and more than one monitor (refused outright); every yield/resume is reported once: "the 2 px edge clip is not in force, so the Windows taskbar can slide up through the island's gaps: <why>" / "the cursor stops 2 px short of the bottom row (<why>)" | sidecar/EdgeClip.cs, main.js sidecar.on('edgeclip') | sidecar/test-sidecar.js | todo |

| BAR-168 | Bar / Put Windows back | Restore order, serialised and idempotent: (0) release the edge clip, then the taskbar's invisibility, (1) remove the app bar, (2) put the auto-hide flag back, (2b) every feature's stop() (each with its own 2.5 s deadline), (3) keyboard hook off (Win key, Alt+Tab, Win+N, Win+A, every chord) and desktop layer detached, (4) log "restored: ..." or "RESTORE INCOMPLETE (<why>): ..." with the recovery command | main.js#restoreShell | lib/supervisor.test.js (partial) | todo |

## Notes for the port
- Pins are READ from Windows (User Pinned\TaskBar .lnk folder plus the Taskband `Favorites` blob; Store-app pins exist only in the blob and need the sidecar `pins` command) and only OVERLAID by Mantle (`<userData>/features/jumplist.json` key `pins` {hide, add, order}). The native bar must read the same overlay file as-is or the user loses pins and drag order. Sidecar: Pins/Taskband reads, Icons.cs, Windows.cs.
- Running-app grouping is AUMID first, exe second, with claims across both keys (Discord Update.exe vs Discord.exe, Store apps under ApplicationFrameHost). Easy to get subtly wrong; port the main.js groupRunning/buildPinned tests as one unit. Native Mantle apps must set per-window AUMIDs (Mantle.Settings etc.) so Mantle's own windows never list as running apps or tray icons; the exe name changes, so the electron.exe tray filter and Quartz path detection need updating.
- The see-through glass is the hardest visual match: the bar has NO DWM glass by design (acrylic ignores tint alpha, blur-behind made a black slab, both measured live). Electron photographs the screen strip behind the pill (light theme, 250 ms loop) or the wallpaper still (dark theme), blurs it itself, and applies a 4.5:1 readability guard plus a dark "one 50 % layer" recipe. A native Mica/Acrylic/composition brush must match the formula (page tests compare pixels: mean <= 1, max <= 4) or the capture approach must be kept. Capture exclusion (WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE) is reference-counted per window and must always be cleared, or the bar is missing from every screenshot the user takes.
- Hover mode uses a tall (640 DIP) window with a window REGION so pop-ups never resize the window; WinUI 3 has no setShape, so it needs SetWindowRgn on the HWND, re-applied after every size change and read back (GetWindowRgnBox) before trusting it. Parking is a move below the screen edge, never Hide(), to keep clicks working. The bar animates INSIDE a fixed window; never SetWindowPos per frame. Pop-up room is a handshake: close always sent before the next open, room freed only after the exit animation, capped at POPOVER_MAX.
- Bottom edge is computed from the physical screen bottom (fractional DPI, e.g. 77.5 px), and placement goes through the sidecar `place` (raw SetWindowPos) because setBounds corrupts the size at fractional scale. The always-on-top band is 'screen-saver' level plus a traywatch re-raise above Explorer's taskbar; re-assert after each reveal/resize and on Explorer restart (TaskbarCreated, handled at most once per 3 s).
- Restore order is load-bearing and idempotent: release the edge clip, then the taskbar's invisibility, then app-bar and auto-hide state. The cursor clip and the invisible taskbar outlive a crashed Mantle, so the native shell needs the same recognise-by-shape cleanup, guard process (TaskbarHide.cs retry ladder 1 s x10, 2, 4, 8, 15, 45 s, 2 min, 5 min) and dirty-start restore.
- Timing contracts to keep: reveal 560 ms spring (41-point linear() curve), exit <= 240 ms (main drops the window at 350 ms), hide 320 ms, grace 180 ms, overshoot 500 ms, hover polls 80/60 ms, fly dwell 400 ms / leave 350 ms, drag 5 px / 24 px / 150 ms settle / 4 s order hold / 1.5 s hold, pop-up blur wait 250 ms, pins debounce 500 ms, pinned push 1 s, icon stat 2 s, tray menu 250 ms, unpin undo 10 min, display burst quiet 400 ms, wake re-place 1.5 s.
- Race fixes easy to lose: "+N apps hidden" is judged only on the landed, revealed bar (live bug "+11" with no icons); backdrop placed against the resting box (the user's "dark outline on the right"); a dropped order outranks stale replies; the CSS class is the truth for up/down; pop-up close precedes a new open. The fit numbers (CELL 36, GAP 6, DIV 7) are duplicated in JS and CSS: use one source natively.
- Live previews: DwmRegisterThumbnail needs the destination to be a top-level window of the calling process. Native WinUI can do this in-process (simpler than Electron's koffi path), but the rects are client-relative physical px and must be cleared on every way out (pop-up close/shrink, bar hide/park, reload, window gone), or a preview stays painted over the icons. The Alt+Tab switcher has its own thumbnail set.
- The bar window is non-focusable at rest; the keyboard is granted only for typing boxes (Wi-Fi password, media queue, jump list menu, Spotify search, calendar day) through `qs.keyboard` and `bar.keyboard.lost`, then handed back to the previous foreground window. WinUI needs a WS_EX_NOACTIVATE bar that becomes activatable on demand. A known Electron quirk puts the bar on the Windows taskbar while it holds the keyboard.
- Outside-click, Esc and app-switch closing of pop-ups depend on the sidecar's global mouse/Esc hook (lib/popwatch.js via Hotkey.cs), armed only while a pop-up is open. Win+A / Win+N: the hook swallows the letter only (never Win) and main sends `bar.winkey`; a hook thread that does not start within 3 s leaves every Win-key feature off. Setting `winKeys` (switcher.json, default on) controls them.
- Game mode is the riskiest function: crash-safe restore through gamemode-restore.json, onlyIf/ecoIf/prioIf guards so a restore never overrides a user change, pid plus start-time identity, 20 retries at 30 s, the never-touch list (must match GameMode.cs), and the Mantle rests hand-off (no capture, no hover poll, no bar reveal while a known game runs). The game process must never be opened for anything but its name (anti-cheat). Keep the pure planner (_gamemode-plan.js) as a separately testable unit.
- Optimistic UI with read-back everywhere in Quick settings (user asked "be way more responsive"): the click shows the new state, the read-back from Windows decides, a refusal springs back with words. Sliders use a coalescing sender (one set in flight, newest waiting; brightness WMI takes 7-353 ms). Levels are 0-100 everywhere. Battery words come from batmeter.dll.mui (build 26200); low/critical levels come from the power plan. Tiles have a paint budget (p95 <= 80 ms from click to second frame) and paint from cached state; no helper call may block the panel opening.
- Network and battery glyphs use SVG masks to knock marks out of the fill (works in light and dark); natively use path geometry with cutouts or font glyphs, not a colour overlay. Title colours use CSS color-mix(in oklab, ...): native needs an OKLab mix helper.
- Wi-Fi password: never logged, never held in state; pass straight to the WLAN profile call and clear the buffer; the field closes if focus is lost. Cast cannot be started natively (no public API): keep the Win+K hand-off. Mobile hotspot is deliberately absent. There is no Do Not Disturb in Mantle's centre (the foot link sends Win+N to Windows' own centre).
- Google Calendar token store: Electron safeStorage on Windows is DPAPI wrapped in Chromium's own format (AES-GCM key kept in Local State); a plain ProtectedData.Protect call will probably NOT decrypt the existing token.bin/client.bin (unverified against the pinned Electron). Plan a one-time re-sign-in or a compatible reader, and delete cache.json when no readable token exists (privacy claim). Loopback sign-in: TcpListener on 127.0.0.1 port 0, one-shot, 5-minute timeout, 404 for favicon, 409 duplicate, constant-time state check. Keep the generation counter that stops a late sync writing after sign-out. The open path does zero IPC and zero network before the first frame. 12-hour h:mm AM times are hard-coded (the user asked for Windows en-US style); dates follow the locale.
- Media: the chip shows picture plus play/pause only (title hidden since 2026-09-24 at the user's request); the source list never reorders under the pointer (400 ms click guard, alphabetical stable order); main enforces the protected-media-apps rule (media-guard.js); controls are named by AUMID and have never been exercised on a real session (live-only). The Spotify section drives the Spotify desktop app over Chrome DevTools Protocol on 127.0.0.1:47292 (_spotify-app.js): a C# WebSocket CDP client or a WebView2/Node fallback is needed; this is the largest single port risk and breaks when Spotify updates. The auto-pause switch is the same feature as Settings' switch (`autopause.switch`): one source of truth.
- Downloads: real file drag-out needs DoDragDrop with a CF_HDROP data object (Electron used webContents.startDrag); rows are addressed by opaque tokens, never paths; names stay out of logs (privacy tests). Helm badge: Helm writes badge.json by tmp+rename; watch the FOLDER and check the pid.
- Tray icons depend on UIA (TrayUia.cs); hidden (non-promoted) icons cannot be clicked at all. Icon plating uses alpha-weighted luminance thresholds 0.75 / 0.22 with >= 6 px coverage; cache bounded at 48. Right-click on a pinned app is Windows' full taskbar menu; jump-list pin/unpin is shown as impossible on purpose. The clock is hand-formatted "h:mm:ss AM/PM" regardless of locale, second-aligned only while the bar is up. Plain-words rewrite table (PLAIN/TECHY) should be a shared, tested helper: every failure path has chip wording (no silent failures).
- Sidecar files this surface depends on: Taskbar.cs, TaskbarHide.cs, Tray*.cs (TrayUia.cs), Icons.cs, Windows.cs, JumpList.cs, Battery.cs, Net*.cs (NetState.cs, Network.cs, NetWifi.cs), Wlan.cs, Bluetooth.cs, BtDevices.cs, Volume*.cs, AudioMixer.cs, Media.cs, QuickSettings.cs, Notifications.cs, PowerMode.cs (and Power.cs/SysPower.cs), GameMode.cs, Downloads.cs, Backdrop.cs, Glass.cs, EdgeClip.cs, Hotkey.cs.
- Facts the inventory found that disagree with code comments (the code wins): notification centre polls every 10 s (a comment in ui/bar/notifications.js says 5 s); a failed media read retries every 5 s while a missing change-event hook polls every 10 s. The unpin Undo backend (jumplist.pin {undo:true}) exists but no UI caller was found; confirm before porting it as user-visible.
- Candidates for a WebView2 fallback: the Spotify CDP section (above) and, if a pixel match to the glass formula proves impractical, the pop-up chrome. Nothing else here is awkward enough to need it; the Wi-Fi password focus dance is the hardest purely native piece.

## Not covered
- Spotify internals: lib/features/_spotify-app.js, _spotify-trim.js, _spotify-revive.js, _spotify-fakecdp.js (CDP calls, hidden start, memory trim, revive after games, close for games). Only what the bar card shows is inventoried; they need their own rows.
- Hot corners (ui/bar/corners.*) and hot-corner reveal exclusion beyond one shared reveal row: shell lane.
- The Settings-page controls for Google Calendar, Game mode, Power mode, Quick links, Bar options: settings lanes (setting keys are recorded in the rows where the code reads them).
- The public privacy page is not in this repo (it is in the portfolio-site repo, static/mantle/privacy.html); the Google Calendar privacy rows cite the page's sections as read by the calendar sub-lane, and the coordinator should re-check them against that repo.
- Launcher, desktop and HUD backdrops; how a top-edge bar and the top bar's peek share the top edge.
- main.js _project-core.js (Win+P pop-up) beyond tile wording, lib/features/apppower.js (App power), _game-watch.js (the top bar's side of the shared game watch).
- Sidecar QuickSettings.cs part fields, hook-health and display-burst internals, OSD feature internals (other lanes map the sidecar itself).
- Test-only hooks (--test-live-bar, --test-dock-extra, bar.test.*, check-reveal.js harness, shoot.js) are listed as single rows or notes, not as user functions; thumbs.js capture()/toBack() are test-only.
- CSS visual detail (exact sizes, colours) beyond the numbers and light/dark differences named in rows; bar.css was read per behaviour, not per rule. Rows were written by reading code, not by running anything; test-column entries were checked by grep, not by running tests.
- Overlap note: a few behaviours (hover band, keep zone, edge clip, Win keys, previews) are described from both the page side and the main-process side in neighbouring rows on purpose; each row is an independently testable statement.
